# Research Bundle for Psalm 27

## Hebrew Lexicon Entries (BDB)

### אוֹרִי
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: אוֹר  

vb. be or become light (cf. Arabic أَوَّرَ enkindle, Aramaic (Nasar.) ܐܘܪ Aph. illumine, & deriv. in Assyrian)— Pf. אוֹר Gn 44:3 +; אֹ֫רוּ 1 S 14:29; Impf. 3 fpl. וַתָּאֹרְנָה 1 S 14:27 Qr; Pt. אוֹר Pr 4:18 (Ges§ 72 R. 1); Imv. fs. א֫וֹרִי Is 60:1;—become light, shine of sun (especially in early morn.) Gn 44:3 (J) 1 S 29:10 Pr 4:18 Is 60:1; of eyes (owing to refreshment) 1 S 14:27(Qr) 29. Impf. וַיֵּאֹר 2 S 2:32; Inf. c. לֵאוֹר Jb 33:30; Pt. נָאוֹר ψ 76:5—become lighted up of day-break 2 S 2:32; by light of life, revival Jb 33:30; by light of glory, enveloped in light ψ 76:5. Pf. וְהֵאִיר Ex 25:37, etc.; Impf. יָאִיר Jb 41:24 +; יָאֵר Nu 6:25 +; וַיָּ֫אֶר ψ 118:27 +, etc.; Imv. הָאֵר ψ 80:20 +, הָאִ֫ירָה ψ 31:17 +; Inf. הָאִיר Gn 1:15 +; Pt. מֵאִיר Pr 29:13; מְאִירַת ψ 19:9; מְאִירוֹת Is 27:11— give light, of sun, moon & stars Gn 1:15, 17 (P), of moon Is 60:19; of pillar of fire (sq. ל) Ex 13:21; 14:20 (both JE) ψ 105:39 Ne 9:12, 19; of sacred lamp Ex 25:37 Nu 8:2 (both P); fig. of the words of God ψ 119:130. lightup, cause to shine, shine, sq. acc. הֵאִירוּ בְרָקִים תֵּבֵל lightnings lighted up the world ψ 77:19; 97:4; abs. הָאָרֶץ הֵאִירָה מִכְּבֹדוֹ Ez 43:2; the earth shined with his glory (of theophany); of leviathan, which makes path shine behind him Jb 41:24; לַיְלָה כַּיּוֹם יָאִיר night shineth as day ψ 139:12. light a lamp ψ 18:29; wood Is 27:11; altar(-fire) Mal 1:10. lighten, of the eyes, הָאִיר עֵינַיִם, subj. י׳, his law, etc. Pr 29:13 ψ 13:4; 19:9 Ezr 9:8. make shine, of face of God יָאֵר י׳ פָּנָיו אֵלֶיךָ Yahweh make his face shine upon thee Nu 6:25 (priest’s blessing), reappearing ψ 31:17 (sq. עַל־), 67:2 (sq. אֶת־), 80:4, 8, 20; 119:135 (sq. ב), Dn 9:17 cf. ψ 4:7; without פָּנִים (sq. ל) ψ 118:27; once, of face of man, Ec 8:1 חָכַמַת אָדָם תָּאִיר פָּנָיו the wisdom of a man lighteth up his face.

### אוֹרִי
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: אוֹר²  

אוֹר121 f. Jb 36:32; Je 13:16; cf. Gn 1:3 light (Assyrian urru = ûru DlW)—abs. Gn 1:3 +; cstr. Ju 16:2 +; sf. אוֹרִי ψ 27:1, etc.; pl. אוֹרִים ψ 136:7;— light as diffused in nature, light of day Gn 1:3, 4, 5 (P) Jb 3:9; 38:19 +. morning light, dawn, אוֹר הַבֹּקֶר light of the morn. Ju 16:2 1 S 14:36; 25:34, 36 2 S 17:22 2 K 7:9 Mi 2:1; א׳ בֹּקֶר 2 S 23:4 (poem of David); עַד־הָא׳ Ju 19:26 (cf. עַד־הַבֹּקֶר v 25); לָאוֹר Jb 24:14; מִן־הָאוֹר עַד מַחֲצִית הַיּוֹם from dawn till mid-day, Ne 8:3 cf. Pr 4:18. light of the heavenly luminaries; א׳ הַלְּבָנָה, ‖ א׳ הַחַמָּה moonlight & sunlight Is 30:26; כּוֹכְבֵי אוֹר stars of light ψ 148:3; מְאוֹרֵי אוֹר luminaries of light Ez 32:8; אוֹרִים = מְאוֹרִים ψ 136:7; so עֲלֵי־אוֹר in sunshine Is 18:4; the sun itself Jb 31:26. daylight אוֹר רְשָׁעִים light of the wicked Jb 38:15 (their work-day being the night); יוֹם אוֹר a day of light Am 8:9 (= a clear, sunshiny day). lightning Jb 36:32; 37:3, 11, 15 cf. Hb 3:11. light of lamp Pr 13:9 Je 25:10; of crocodile’s hot breath Jb 41:10. light of life א׳ חַיִּים Jb 33:30 ψ 56:14; cf. אוֹר Jb 3:16, 20. light of prosperity Jb 22:28; 30:26 ψ 97:11 La 3:2. light of instruction נֵר מִצְוָה וְתוֹרָה אוֹר Pr 6:23 the commandment is a lamp and instruction a light; so the Messian. servant is אוֹר גּוֹיִם Is 42:6; 49:6; cf. א׳ עַמִּים Is 51:4; the advent of Mess. is shining of great light Is 9:1(×2). light of face אוֹר פָּנִים = bright, cheerful face (of men) Jb 29:24; betokening king’s favour Pr 16:15 (cf. א׳ עֵינַיִם ψ 38:11); of God = his shining, enlightening, favouring face ψ 4:7; 44:4; 89:16. Yahweh is א׳ יִשְׂרָאֵל Is 10:17, as source of enlightenment & prosperity; light & salvation ψ 27:1; light to guide Mi 7:8 cf. ψ 43:3; everlasting light of Zion, instead of sun & moon Is 60:19, 20; house of Jacob is to walk in his light Is 2:5.

**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: מָעוֹז  

place of safety, strong-hold. shelter, refuge. protection.

### מָעוֹז
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: מָעוֹז²  

n.m. Ju 6:26 place or means of safety, protection (MT sfs. as if from עזז, but prob. erron., cf. BuhlLex 13 Ges§ 85 k);—מ׳ abs. Ju 6:26 +, cstr. Is 30:2 +; sf. מָעוּזִי 2 S 22:33 (but read הַמְּאַזְּרֵנִי as ‖ ψ 18:33), מָעוּזִּי ψ 31:5 +, מָעֻזּי Is 27:5 +, מָעוּזּוֹ ψ 52:9 +, מָעֻזֹּה Dn 11:10, etc. (read proposes מְעוֹזִי, etc.); pl. מָעֻזִּים v 38, 39, sf. מָעֻזְּנֶיהָ Is 23:11, read מָעֻזֶּיהָ (Ges§ 20 o CheHeb. Hpt. al.; del. d. f. in all, v. supr.);— lit. place of safety 2 S 22:33, hence (c. collat. idea of strength, as if from עזז) = fastness Ju 6:26; Is 23:11, 14 (in v 14 (CheHeb. Hpt. proposes מְחוֹזְכֶם your city), Ez 24:25; 30:15; Dn 11:7, 10, 19, הַמִּקְדָּשׁ הַמּ׳ v 31, מִבְצְרֵי מָעֻזִּים v 39; מ׳ הַיָּם i.e. harbour Is 23:4, but del. Ol Du CheHpt.; מ׳ מֵאוֹיֵב Na 3:11; cf. עָרֵי מ׳ Is 17:9. fig., of God (י׳) as refuge Na 1:7 ψ 27:1; 31:5; 37:39; 52:9; Is 25:4(×2); Jo 4:16, י׳ עֻזִּי וּמָעֻזִּי וּמְנוּסִי Je 16:19, מ׳ יְשׁוּעוֹת מְשִׁיחוֹ ψ 28:8 (‖ עָז־); so צוּר מ׳ Is 17:10 ψ 31:3, אֱלֹהֵי מ׳ 43:2; also יַחֲזֵק בְּמָעֻוִּי Is 27:5 let him lay hold of my refuge (me as refuge); cf. חֶדְוַת י׳ הִיא מָעֻזְּכֶם Ne 8:10, מ׳ לַתֹּם דֶּרֶךְ י׳ Pr 10:29. of a heathen god, אֱלוֹהַּ מָעֻזִּים Dn 11:38 (read מְחֹזִים cities Che Is 23:1 Heb. Hpt.). fig. of human protection, מ׳ פַּרְעֹה Is 30:2, 3 (both ‖ צֵל מִצְרַיִם); אֶפְרַיִם מ׳ רֹאשִׁי ψ 60:9 = 108:9, i.e. my helmet; also (of angel) Dn 11:1 (‖ מַחֲזִיק).

### חָזָה
**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: חזה  

to see, behold. he saw, beheld, perceived; he prophesied. he predicted; he forecast. was forecast.

**Etymology**: [Aram.-Syr. חֲזָא (= he saw), Ugar. ḥdy. (= to see), Arab. ḥazi (= astrologer).  

**Derivatives**: Derivatives: הִתְחַזּוּת, חַזָּאוּת, חַזַּאי, חָזוּי, חִזּוּי, חָזוֹן, חָזוּת ᴵ, חָזּוּת ᴵᴵ, חֹזִי, חֲזִיָּה, חִזָּיּוֹן, חֲזִית, מַחֲזֶה, מֶחֱזָה, מַחֲזִיָּה, מַחֲזִית, תַּחֲזִית. cp. חַזָּן.

### חָזָה
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: חָזָה  

vb. (almost wholly poet.) see, behold (Aramaic ܚܙܳܐ, חֲזָא, see, perceive with the eyes; Palm. Ethp. אתחזי = ἔδοξεν Reckendorf ZMG, 1888, 397; Arabic حَزَى, perceive with the inner vision, only; حَازٍ astronomer, astrologer)— Pf. ח׳ ψ 58:11 + 6 times; חָזִיתָ Pr 22:29; 29:20; חָזִית Is 57:8; חָזִיתִי Jb 15:17; חֲזִיתִךָ ψ 63:3; חָ֥זוּ Jb 24:1 + 5 times; חֲזִיתֶם Jb 27:12 Ez 13:7, 8; Impf. יֶחֱזֶה Nu 24:4 + 2 times; 3 fs. וְתַ֫חַז Mi 4:11; 2 ms. תֶּחֱזֶה Ex 18:21; 1 s. אֶחֱזֶה ψ 17:15 + 4 times; אָ֫חַז Jb 23:9; pl. יֶחֱזוּ Is 26:11 + 4 times; יֶחֱזָ֑יוּן Is 26:11; תֶּחֱזֶ֫ינָת Is 33:17 + 2 times; תֶּחֱזוּ Is 30:10 Ct 7:1; 1 pl. וְנֶחֱזֶת Ct 7:1; Imv. חֲזֵה Is 33:20; 48:6; חֲזוּ ψ 46:9 Is 30:10; Inf. cstr. חֲזוֹת ψ 27:4 Ez 21:34; Pt. חֹזֶה Ez 12:27; pl. חֹזִים Is 47:13 Ez 13:9, 16; 22:28. (All other ptc. forms are used as nouns, v. חֹזֶה.) see, behold: with the eye, acc. rei ψ 58:9, 11 Pr 22:29; 29:20 Is 33:20; 57:8; God in theophany Ex 24:11 (JE); God, after death, apart from the flesh Jb 19:26, 27; the face of God ψ 11:7; 17:15 (after death). subj. the eyes themselves; man’s eyes: תֶּחֱזֶינָה עֵינֶיךָ thine eyes will behold Is 33:17; also of God’s eyes: ψ 11:4; 17:2. with בְּ look on (intensely, with gratification); a damsel Ct 7:1, 1; Zion Mi 4:11; נֹעַם י׳ in the temple ψ 27:4; the work of God Jb 36:25; with investigation בַּכּוֹכָבִים on the stars, as stargazers, in astrology Is 47:13. see as a seer in the ecstatic state, with acc. of the vision seen: חזון Is 1:1 Ez 12:27; 13:16; מחזה Nu 24:4, 16 (JE; poet., Balaam); מחזה שׁוא Ez 13:7; משּׂא Is 13:1 Hb 1:1; משׂאות שׁוא La 2:14; שׁוא Ez 13:6, 9, 23; 21:34; 22:28 La 2:14; כזב Ez 13:8; שֶׁקֶר Zc 10:2; obj. deceitful things Is 30:10; דבר(ים) Is 2:1 Am 1:1 Mi 1:1; with לְ, be a seer, for any one Is 30:10; see a vision for one La 2:14, 14. see, perceive: with the intelligence, abs. Jb 34:32 Pr 24:32; acc. rei Is 48:6; Yahweh in his temple ψ 63:3; in his providential workings Jb 23:9 ψ 46:9 Is 26:11(×2). see, by experience abs. Jb 27:12; acc. rei Jb 15:17; 24:1. provide, תחזה מכל העם אנשׁי חיל Ex 18:21 provide from all the people men of ability (E). —בֵּית אֲבָנִים יֶחֱזֶה Jb 8:17 is difficult: RV renders he (it) beholdeth the place of stones (the root of the plant personified) Hengst, cf. Reuss al. after 𝔖 𝔗. Other renderings are: pierceth (cf. חָזַז) Bö Ew Hi (יְחַזֶּה), Di De MV; between (v. p. 108) the stones it liveth (יִחְיֶה) 𝔊 Siegf.

### סֻכָּה
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: סֻכָּה  

n.f. thicket, booth (proposes of interwoven boughs cf. Ne 8:15);—abs. ס׳ Is 1:8 +; cstr. סֻכַּת Am 9:11; sf. סֻכָּתוֹ Jb 36:29 ψ 18:12, and so read ‖ 2 S 22:12 (for MT סֻכּוֹת; De Hup-Now HPS); usually pl. סֻכֹּת Gn 33:17 +; סֻכּוֹת 2 S 11:11; +;— thicket, lurking-place of lions Jb 38:40 (cf. [סֹךְ] 1). booth, rude or temporary shelter, for cattle Gn 33:17 (J; distinct from בַּיִת), but also for warriors in the field 2 S 11:11 1 K 20:12, 16; for watchers in vineyards Is 1:8 (sim.) Jb 27:18 (sim. of frailty), for man’s shelter from sun Jon 4:5, cf. (fig.) Is 4:6 ψ 31:21, prob. also 27:5 (v. [סֹךְ]); poet. of fallen house (dynasty) of David Am 9:11; of clouds as (temporary) enclosure (AV ‘pavilion’) of י׳ in storm ψ 18:12 = 2 S 22:12 Jb 36:29. specif. of booths, made of boughs, in which people lived at harvest-feast Lv 23:42(×2), 43 (H), Ne 8:14, 15, 16, 17(×2), hence called חַג הַסֻּכּוֹת Dt 16:13, 16; 31:10; Lv 23:34 (P), Zc 14:16, 18, 19; 2 Ch 8:13; Ezr 3:4.

### תְּרוּעָה
**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: תְּרוּעָה  

shout, cry, alarm. a succession of tremulous notes of the ‘shofar’ or trumpet.

**Etymology**: [Verbal n. of הֵרִיעַ (= he shouted, gave a blast), Hiph. of רוע. For the form of this word see תְּבוּנָה.]  

**Derivatives**: Derivatives: תרע, תּוֹרְעָנִי.

### תְּרוּעָה
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: תְּרוּעָה  

n.f. shout or blast of war, alarm, or joy;—ת׳ Lv 23:24 +; cstr. תְּרוּעַת Je 4:19 +;— alarm of war, war-cry, Jos 6:5, 20; Je 20:16; Ez 21:27; Am 1:14; 2:2; Zp 1:16; Jb 39:25; ת׳ מִלְחָמָה Je 4:19; 49:2; ת׳ מֶלֶךְ battle-cry of kingNu 23:21 (poem in JE). blast for march: תקע ת׳ Nu 10:5, 6(×2) (P; with חֲצֹצְרוֹת; hence) חֲצֹצְרוֹת הַתּ׳ Nu 31:6 (P) 2 Ch 13:12; on day of atonement שׁוֹפַר ת׳ Lv 25:9 (H); 1st of mo. זִכְרוֹן ת׳ 23:24 (P; בַּחֹדֶשׁ הַשְּׁבִיעִי בְּאֶחָד לַחֹדֶשׁ), יוֹם תֹ Nu 29:1 (P); gen. צִלְצְלֵי ת׳ ψ 150:5. shout of joy with religious impulse, 1 S 4:5, 6(×2); 2 S 6:15 = 1 Ch 15:28; 2 Ch 15:14; Ezr 3:11, 12, 13; קוֹל ת׳ הַשִּׂמְחָה Ezr 3:13; in public worship gen. Jb 33:26; especially + musical service ψ 33:3; 47:6; זִבְחֵי ת׳ 27:6, יֹדְעֵי ת׳ 89:16. shout of joy, in gen. Jb 8:21 (‖שְׂחוֹק).

### לוּלֵא
**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: לוּלֵא  

if not, were it not, unless.

**Etymology**: [Formed with change of ū-ō to ū-ē (vowel dissimilation) from לוּלֹא, a compound of לוּ (= would that), and לֹא (= not). For a similar vowel dissimilation cp. אִלּוּלֵא.]

### לוּלֵא
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: לוּלֵא  

Gn 43:10; Ju 14:18; 2 S 2:27; ψ 27:13, elsewhere לוּלֵי10 if not, unless (from לוּ if, and לֵא, by dissim. (Köii 236, 489) for לֹא not; cf. Arabic لَوْلَا ), the neg. of לוּ, and used similarly:— sq. pf., Ju 14:18 לולא חרשׁתם … לא מצאתם unless ye had ploughed with my heifer, ye would not have found out my riddle, 1 S 25:34 (second כי resumptive: כִּי 1 d), ψ 106:23; with apod. introd. by כִּי עַתָּה Gn 31:42; 43:10; by אָז 2 S 2:27 (כי resumptive); by כִּמְעַט Is 1:9; with an aposiop. ψ 27:13 if I had not believed …! sq. impf. Dt 32:27 אמרתי … לולי אגור I should have said, &c. … except I dreaded, &i>c. sq. ptcp., 2 K 3:14. without a verb, ψ 94:17 (apod. כמעט), 119:92 (apod. אָז). In the later language, ψ 124:1, 2 לוּלֵי י׳ שֶׁ- (apod. אֲזַי) except that … (cf. Aramaic ܕ ? … ܐܶܠܘܽ ܠܴܐ ψ 106:23, אִילּוּלֵי … דִּי ψ 27:13 𝔗).—Read also לוּלֵי for אוּלַי in Nu 22:33 (apod. כִּי עַתָּה). See further on לוּ and לוּלֵא Dr§§ 139–145 Köiii. 487 f. 565.

### אֶרֶץ חַיִּים
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: אֶ֫רֶץ  

n.f. Gn 10:11 & (seld.) m. Ez 21:24 earth, land (Ph. MI ארץ, Assyrian irṣitu COTGloss, Arabic أَرْضٌ, Sab. ארץֿ e.g. Os 9 DHMZMG 1875, 594, 614; Sem. Sprachf. 12, cf. PräBAS i. 374 n., Aramaic אֲרַע, ܐܰܪܥܳܐ )—א׳ abs. Gn 1:24 +; cstr. 2:11 +; אָ֑רֶץ 1:10 +; c. art. always הָאָ֫רֶץ 1:11 +; c. ה—ָloc. אַ֫רְצָה 11:31 +, (poet. = אָרֶץ Jb 34:13; cf. 1 S 25:23 1 K 1:31); sf. אַרְצִי 20:15 + אַרְצְךָ (אַרְצֶ֑ךָ) 12:1 +, etc.; pl. אֲרָצוֹת Je 28:8 + 65 times; cstr. אַרְצוֹת Ez 39:27 + 6 times; sf. אַרְצֹתָם Gn 10:5 + 2 times;— earth, whole earth (opp. to a part) Gn 18:18, 25; 22:18 (= הָאֲדָמָה 12:3) Je 25:26, 29, 30; 26:6 Is 37:16, 20 = 2 K 19:15, 19 Zc 4:10, 14 +. earth, opp. to heaven, sky Gn 1:2 Ex 20:4 Dt 5:8; 30:19 Ju 5:4 La 2:1 Is 37:16 = 2 K 19:15 ψ 146:6 1 Ch 21:16; 29:11 2 Ch 2:11 +; as permanent Ec 1:4; built on foundations, or pillars 1 S 2:8 ψ 104:5 Jb 38:4 Is 48:13; 51:13, 16 cf. also Is 24:18 ψ 82:5; firm, so that its shaking is something terrible, & token of terrible power 1 S 14:5 2 S 22:8 = ψ 18:8 Jb 9:6 cf. ψ 46:3 & v 7; so also Am 8:8 Is 2:19, 21; 24:18, 19, 20 ψ 60:4; 77:19; 99:1; 114:7; as hung on nothing Jb 26:7; with waters under it Ex 20:4 = Dt 5:8 cf. Gn 7:11; personified, especially as addressed, called to witness, etc. Dt 32:1 Je 6:19; 22:29 Is 1:2 Mi 1:2 Jb 16:18. earth = inhabitants of earth Gn 6:11; 11:1 1 K 2:2; 10:24 ψ 33:8; 66:4 + cf. תֵּבֵל א׳ Pr 8:31 Jb 37:12. land = country, territory, א׳ שִׁנְעָר Gn 10:10, א׳ מִצְרַיִם 21:21; cf. also 10:11; 11:28, 31; 13:10; 47:6, 27; 50:8 Is 7:18; 23:1, 13; 27:13 ψ 78:12 Je 25:20 1 Ch 1:43; personif. Is 62:4 Ec 10:16, 17. district, region Gn 19:28; 22:2 Jos 11:3 ψ 42:7. tribal territory Dt 34:2 Ju 21:21 1 S 9:4, 16; 13:7 1 K 15:20 Is 8:23; and still smaller territories 1 S 9:4, 5. piece of ground Gn 23:15. specif. land of Canaan, or Israel Gn 11:31; 12:1, 5, 6, 7; 31:3 Lv 14:34 Dt 17:14; 18:9 2 K 5:2, 4 +; especially obj. of יָרַשׁ possess Dt 3:20 + often Dt ψ al.; so after נָחַל Jos 19:49 +; נָתַן Dt 1:21 +. f. = inhabitants of land Lv 19:29 Ez 14:13 + cf. Dt 24:4 Zc 12:12 etc. g. used even of Sheʾôl Jb 10:21, 22 (cf. Assyrian irṣit la târat, land without return, in Descent of Ishtar, v. Jr 10.65); v. also ψ 139:15 Is 44:23. ground, surface of ground = אֲדָמָה q.v. Gn 1:26, 30; 18:2; 33:3; 38:9 Ex 4:3; 16:14 Ru 2:10 1 S 5:4 + very often in S. soil, as productive = אֲדָמָה Gn 1:11, 12 Lv 19:9; 25:19; 26:4 cf. Nu 14:7, 8 Is 36:17 = 2 K 18:32 ψ 72:6, 16; 107:34, 35 Ezr 9:12 Ne 9:35. אֶרֶץ in phrases: people of the land עַם־הָא׳ of non-Israelites Gn 23:7, 12, 13 (P) Nu 14:9 (JE); as well as Isr. Lv 20:4 (H) 2 K 15:5; 16:15; 21:24(×2); especially common people, opp. officials, princes Lv 4:27 (P) Ez 7:27 2 K 11:18, 19. in measurements of distance, כִּבְרַת הָא׳ the space or distance of country (v. כּברה) Gn 35:16, so כִּבְרַת א׳ some distance Gn 48:7 2 K 5:19. אֶרֶץ הַמִּישׁוֹר the country of the plain, level or plain country Dt 4:33 Je 48:21; א׳ מִישׁוֹר fig. ψ 143:10 (but read אֹרַח 𝔖 Bi Gr Che, cf. ψ 27:11). א׳חַיִּים land of the living ψ 27:13; א׳ הַחַיִּים ψ 142:6 +. קְצֵה הָא׳ end(s) of the earth Is 42:10; 43:6 (‖ רָחוֹק) ψ 135:7 Pr 17:24, so אַפְסֵי א׳ Pr 30:4 +; קְצוֹת הָא׳ Is 40:28; 41:5, 9. pl. אֲרָצוֹת is almost wholly late, Je 16:15 + 6 times Je; 23 times Ez; Is 36:20; 37:11 = 2 K 18:35; 19:11 (Is 37:18 read הַגּוֹיִם v. Che Di & cf. 2 K 19:17); 22 times Chr; Dn 9:7; 11:40, 42; ψ 105:44; 106:27; 107:3; 116:9; besides these only P Gn 10:5, 20, 31 Lv 26:36, 39, except Gn 26:3, 4 (JR) 41:54 (JE); it denotes lands, countries, often in contrast to Canaan, lands of the nations, etc., v. especially abs. Ez 20:32; 22:4; = the various petty divisions of Canaan afterward united under Israel’s control Gn 26:3, 4, cf. 1 Ch 13:2 אַרְצוֹת יִשְׂרָאֵל, 2 Ch 11:23 אַרְצוֹת יְהוּדָה.

### אֶרֶץ חַיִּים
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: חַי²  

adj. alive, living;—ח׳ Gn 25:6 +; חָ֑י Gn 3:20 +, חֵי Am 8:14 + 15 times (Ew§ 329 explains as cstr., but Ges§ 93, R 7 n. al. as contracted abs.); f. חַיָּה Gn 1:20 +; pl. חַיִּים ψ 116:9 +;— of God, as the living one, the fountain of life אֵל חַי Jos 3:10 (J), Ho 2:1 ψ 42:3; 84:3; אלהים חי 2 K 19:4, 16 = Is 37:4, 17; אלהים חיים Dt 5:23 1 S 17:26, 36 Je 10:10; 23:36; חי יהוה Yahweh is living ψ 18:47 = 2 S 22:47; גאלי חי my avenger is living Jb 19:25; the formula of the oath is חַי י׳ Ju 8:19 Ru 3:13 1 S 14:39, 45; 19:6; 20:3, 21; 25:34; 26:10, 16; 28:10; 29:6 2 S 4:9; 12:5; 14:11; 15:21 1 K 1:29; 2:24; 17:1, 12; 18:10; 22:14 = 2 Ch 18:13, 2 K 5:16, 20 Ho 4:15 Je 4:2; 5:2; 12:16; 16:14, 15; 23:7, 8; 38:16; חי אל Jb 27:2; חי האלהים 2 S 2:27; חי אדני י׳ Je 44:26; חי י׳ צבאות 1 K 18:15 2 K 3:14; as used by God Himself it is חי אנכי Dt 32:40, elsewhere חי אני Nu 14:21, 28 (P) Is 49:18; Je 22:24; 46:18 Ez 5:11; 14:16, 18, 20; 16:48; 17:16, 19; 18:3; 20:3, 31, 33; 33:11, 27; 34:8; 35:6, 11 Zp 2:9, cf. also חֵי דרך באר שׁבע, חֵי אלהיך דן Am 8:14; with the exception of בְּחֵי הָעֹולָם by him who liveth for ever Dn 12:7, חֵי is always (as an artificial distinction of scribes) used of non-sacred oaths, v. b. of man; אָדָם חַי a living man La 3:39, in antith. מֵת: חי חי Is 38:19; הַחַי collective Ec 7:2; Absalom 2 S 19:7; Naboth 1 K 21:15; a son or lad 2 S 12:21 1 K 3:22(×2), 23(×2), 25, 26(×2), 27; 17:23; usually pl. חיים alive, living Nu 16:30, 33 (J), 17:13 (P), Dt 4:4; 5:3 Is 8:19 ψ 55:16; 124:3 Pr 1:12 Ec 4:2(×2), 15; 6:8; 9:4, 5 Ru 2:20; taking prisoners alive Jos 8:23 (J) 1 S 15:8 1 K 20:18(×2) 2 K 7:12; 10:14(×2) 2 Ch 25:12; living (prosperously) 1 S 25:6 (We reads לָחִי = לְאָחִי (v. Klo Dr) to my brother, after 𝔙 fratribus meis); elsewhere in phrase כל־הימים אשׁר הֵם (אַתֶּם) היים על (פני) האדמה all the days that they (ye) live upon (the face of) the land Dt 4:10; 12:1; 31:13 1 K 8:40 = 2 Ch 6:31. Note phrases: עוֹד חַי (ים) yet alive Gn 25:6; 43:7, 27, 28; 45:28; 46:30 (J), 45:3, 26 Ex 4:18 (E), Dt 31:27 1 Sa 20:14 2 S 12:22; 18:14 1 K 20:32; בהיות חי 2 S 12:18 1 K 12:6 2 Ch 10:6; אֶרֶץ (הַ)חַיִים land of the living Is 38:11; 53:8 Je 11:19 Ez 26:20; 32:23, 24, 25, 26, 27, 32 ψ 27:13; 52:7; 142:6 Jb 28:13; ארצות הח׳ ψ 116:9; ספר ח׳ ψ 69:29; צרור הח׳ bundle of the living 1 S 25:29; אור הח׳ light of the living Jb 33:30 ψ 56:14. In the oath by life of men חי is pointed always חֵי: חֵי אדני המלך as my lord the king liveth 2 S 15:21; חֵי פרעה Gn 42:15, 16 (E); חֵי נַפְשְׁךָ 1 S 1:26; 17:55 2 S 14:19; חַי י׳ וְחֵי נַפְשְׁךָ as Yahweh liveth and as thy soul (or thyself) liveth 1 S 20:3; 25:26 2 K 2:2, 4, 6; 4:30 חַיֶּךָ וְחֵי נ׳ 2 S 11:11 (but on text v. We Dr). of animals, alive, living: ox Ex 21:35; 22:3 (E); goat Lv 16:10, 20, 21 (P); bird Lv 14:4, 6(×2), 7, 51, 53; dog, לְכֶלֶב חַי הוּא טוֹב מִן־הָאַרְיֵה הַמֵּת Ec 9:4 (comp. Arabic prov. Kalb ḥay wa-lâ maiyit, a living dog and no dead lion Wetzst Verhand. Berl. Anthrop. Ges 1878, 388); reptiles Gn 1:28 (P); animals in general Gn 9:3 (P); בשׂר חי living, raw flesh Lv 13:10, 14, 15(×2), 16 (P) 1 S 2:15. animals and man, phrases for either or both: כל חי Gn 3:20; 8:21 (J) Jb 12:10; 28:21; 30:23 ψ 143:2; 145:16; כל החי Gn 6:19 (P); נֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה Gn 1:20, 24, 30 (P) 2:7, 19 (J); כל נֶפֶשׁ חַיָּה Gn 9:12, 15, 16 (P) Ez 47:9; כל נפשׁ החיה Gn 1:21; 9:10 Lv 11:10, 46 (P). (dub.) of vegetation, as thorns, green, ψ 58:10 (Ges Ew Ol Pe, but De Ri Che Bae raw flesh, v. c supr. ad fin.) of water, flowing, fresh מים חיים Gn 26:19 (J), Lv 14:5, 6, 50, 51, 52; 15:13 Nu 19:17 (P), Je 2:13; 17:13 Zc 14:8 Ct 4:15. (dub.) lively, active: אישׁ חַי an active man 2 S 23:20 (but Qr אישׁ חַיִל is to be preferred); איבי חיים mine enemies are lively ψ 38:20 (RV, but Houb Ol Ew Hu. Che al. read חִנָּם, ‖ שׁקר). reviving: כָּעֵת חַיָּה at the time (when it is) reviving, the spring Gn 18:10, 14 (J), 2 K 4:16, 17.

n.f. living thing, animal (Zinj. wild beast DHMSendsch. 34)—ח׳ Gn 8:17 +; cstr. חַיַּת Is 57:10 +; old case ending (poetic) חַיְתוֹ Gn 1:24 ψ 50:10; 79:2; 104:11, 20 Is 56:9(×2) Zp 2:14; sf. חַיָּתִי ψ 143:3, etc.; pl. חַיּוֹת Lv 14:4 + 12 times— animal, as a living, active being: in general, Gn 8:17 Lv 11:2, 27, 47(×2) Nu 35:3 (P) ψ 104:25 Is 46:1 Dn 8:4. wild animals, on account of their vital energy and activity Gn 7:14, 21; 8:1, 19; 9:5 (P) Lv 17:13; 25:7 (H) Jb 37:8 ψ 148:10 Is 40:16 Ez 14:15; 33:27 Zp 2:14, 15; ח׳ קנה ψ 68:31 wild animal of the reeds; ח׳ רעה evil beast Gn 37:20, 33 (JE) Lv 26:6 (H) Ez 5:17; 14:15, 21; 34:25; ח׳ טמאה Lv 5:2 (P) unclean beast; ח׳ הארץ Gn 1:25, 30; 9:2, 10(×2) (P) 1 S 17:46 Ez 29:5; 32:4; 34:28 Jb 5:22; חיתו ארץ Gn 1:24 (P) ψ 79:2; ח׳ השׂדה Ex 23:11, 29 (covt. code) Lv 26:22 (H) Dt 7:22 2 S 21:10, 2 K 14:9 = 2 Ch 25:18, Ho 2:14, 20; 4:3; 13:8 Is 43:20 Je 27:6; 28:14 Ez 38:20; 39:4 Jb 5:23; 39:15; כל ח׳ השׂדה Gn 2:19, 20; 3:1, 14 (J) Jb 40:20 Je 12:9 Ez 31:6, 13; 34:5, 8; 39:17; חיתו שׂדי Is 56:9 ψ 104:11; חיתו (ב)יער Is 56:9 ψ 50:10; 104:20; פְּרִיץ חיות Is 35:9 destroyer among beasts. living beings, of the cherubic chariot Ez 1:5, 13(×2), 14, 15(×2), 19(×2), 20, 21, 22; 3:13; 10:15, 17, 20. life, only in late poetry, ψ 143:3 Jb 33:18, 20, 22, 28; 36:14 Ez 7:13(×2), and (dub.) ψ 74:19; 78:50. appetite, activity of hunger: חית כפרים appetite of young lions Jb 38:39. revival, renewal: חַיַּת יָדֵךְ מָצָאתְ thou didst find renewal of thy strength (re-invigoration) Is 57:10; v. חָיָה 2.

### אֶרֶץ חַיִּים
**Lexicon**: BDB Dictionary
**Vocalized**: חַיָּה²  

n.f. (si vera l.) community (= fem. of ii. חַי according to NöZMG, 1886, 176)—וְחַיַּת פְּלִשְׁתִּים חֹנָה and a community of Philistines (i.e. a group of allied families, making a raid together) was encamping 2 S 23:13 (= מַחֲנֵה 1 Ch 11:15); perhaps also ψ 68:11 (cf. infr.) —Several other passages are dub.: וַיֵּאָסְפוּ פְלִשְׁתִּים לַחַיָּה 2 S 23:11 assembled into a troop, or by bands (RV, but neither rend. justif.: Bö Ew We Klo Dr rightly read לֶ֫חְיָה to Leḥi, cf. Ju 15:9; wd. om. by accident in ‖ 1 Ch 11:13 Dr); חַיָּֽתְךָ יָֽשְׁבוּ־בָהּ ψ 68:11 thy troop dwelt in it (Thes SS RVm Hi Ri Che, but 𝔊 𝔖 Jer Pe thy (living) creatures, of the people; Hup id., ref. to quails; improb.); אַל־תִּתֵּן לְחַיַּת נֶפֶשׁ תּוֹרֶךָ חַיַּת עֲנִיֶּיךָ אַל־תִּשְׁכַּח לָנֶ֫צַח ψ 74:19 give not the soul of thy turtle-dove to the wild beasts, the life of thine afflicted do not forget for ever: RV Hi Pe Bae Sch render thus, giving חית diff. meanings in the two clauses, the former being archaic fem. abs. Ges§ 80, 2. R. 2; text doubtless corrupt, read poss. למות Schr Ri, for לחית, or לשׁחת Kroch; Gr either of these; Che either, or better לחרב.

### קַוָּה
**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: קוה ᴵ  

to wait for (the orig. meaning prob. was ‘to twist, stretch’, whence ‘to be stretched, be strained’, whence ‘to await tensely’). he waited for, expected, hoped for; he lay in wait for. was expected, was hoped for. he gave hope to, encouraged.

**Etymology**: [Related to Arab. qawiya (= was strong), quwwa (= strength; also ‘strand of a rope’), Syr. קַוִּי (= he endured, remained, waited), qūyā (= loom), Aram. קַוָּא (= thread), pl. קַוִּין (= ‘threads’, esp. ‘spider’s web’), Akka. qu’ū (= to wait), qū (= cord). Base of קַו ᴵ.] — The Qal occurs only in the act. part. קוֶֹה (q.v.).  

**Derivatives**: Derivatives: קַו ᴵ, קִוּוּי ᴵ, קְוִיָּה ᴵ, מִקְוֶה ᴵᴵ, מְקֻוֶּה ᴵᴵ, תִּקְוָה ᴵ, תִּקְוָה ᴵᴵ.  

---

### קַוָּה
**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: קוה ᴵᴵ  

to collect (water). he collected. was collected (in the Bible occurring only Gen. 1:9 and Jer. 3:17). he collected, gathered (water). was collected, was gathered (said of water). (of s.m.).

**Etymology**: [JAram. אַקְוִי (= he collected). According to some scholars related to Syr. קְבָא (= was collected, ran together), prob. also related to Ethiop. qabawa (= he suffered from dropsy).]  

**Derivatives**: Derivatives: קוה ᴵᴵᴵ, קָווּי, קִוּוּי ᴵᴵ, קִוּוּיָה, הַקְוָאָה, הִקָּווּת, מִקְוֶה ᴵ, מִקְוָה.  

---

### קַוָּה
**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: קוה ᴵᴵᴵ  

to call, invoke. ). (see קוה ᴵᴵ).

**Etymology**: [Special sense development of קוה ᴵᴵ. This meaning is obvious in Ps. 40:2, where the action expressed by קִוִּיתִי must be related to the noun שַׁוְעָתִי, ‘my cry’. For the relation between ‘call’ and ‘collect’, cp. the nouns קוֹל and קָהָל.]  

---

### קַוָּה
**Lexicon**: Klein Dictionary
**Vocalized**: קוֶֹה  

occurring only 6 times in the Bible (always in the pl.), usually regarded as the act. part. Qal of קוה ᴵ and accordingly rendered by ‘waiting for, hopeful’. However, it is more probable that קוֶֹה is the act. part. of קוה ᴵᴵᴵ and means ‘he that calls or invokes’. cp. קַו ᴵᴵ.

**Etymology**: [For other verbs which have only the actor passparticiple form in the Qal and are usually identical in meaning to the partof the Pi‘el cp. בָּרוּךְ, דּוֹבֵר (see דבר ᴵ), שׁוֹחֵר, (Pr. 11:27; see שׁחר ᴵ), שׁוֹקֵר (Pr. 17:4; see שׁקר). cp. קַו ᴵᴵ.]

### קַוָּה

### Prose Insights

#### 1. The Immediacy of Light vs. The Darkness of Fear
*Addresses verses*: 27:1  

Psalm 27:1 opens with a rare direct predication: 'The LORD is my light.' The concordance reveals that 'light' is typically a vehicle for guidance (2 Kings 8:19) or a contrast to the 'darkening' of counsel (Job 38:2). However, in Psalm 27, the metaphor is absolute—YHWH *is* the light, not merely the sender of it. This choice of vehicle is polemical against the 'darkness' implied by the surrounding enemies. Where Job 3:9 hopes for light in vain, the psalmist possesses it as an ontological reality. This metaphor dissolves the cognitive and emotional paralysis of fear; just as darkness cannot exist in the presence of light, fear cannot coexist with the realization of YHWH as one's personal illumination.

---

#### 2. Zoomorphic Enemies: The Terror of Devouring
*Addresses verses*: 27:2  

The enemies in verse 2 are not merely military opponents; they are depicted through the vehicle of 'devouring' (*akal*) flesh, transforming them into predatory beasts. The concordance links this imagery to wild beasts (Jer 12:9) and the 'mouth' of the sword (2 Kings 10:25). By selecting the vehicle of predation rather than simple warfare, the poet heightens the threat from political to existential—they do not just want to defeat him; they want to consume him. This zoomorphism sets up the dramatic reversal: these 'beasts' do not feast; instead, they 'stumble and fall' (v2), their predatory agility failing them in the presence of the psalmist's 'Light.'

---

#### 3. Architectural Intimacy: House, Booth, and Rock
*Addresses verses*: 27:4, 27:5  

The psalm shifts from the battlefield to the sanctuary, utilizing three distinct architectural vehicles: 'House' (v4), 'Booth/Shelter' (*sukkah*, v5), and 'Rock' (v5). While 'House' suggests permanence and dynasty (1 Kings 2:33), the *sukkah* is a fragile, temporary structure. The insight lies in the paradox: God hides the psalmist in a 'booth' on the 'day of evil.' Unlike a 'fortress' (v1) which relies on thick walls, the *sukkah* relies entirely on the host's presence. The concordance shows *sukkah* is often a shelter from elements (Isa 4:6), but here it is a hiding place from enemies. The progression is telling: from the public 'House' of worship to the secret 'Booth' of concealment, finally elevating the psalmist onto an inaccessible 'Rock.'

---

#### 4. The Drama of the Face: Presence as Hazard and Hope
*Addresses verses*: 27:8, 27:9  

The metaphor of the 'face' (*panim*) dominates the psalm's center (v8-9). The concordance illustrates that 'setting the face' indicates determination (Jer 42:15) or hostility (Ezek 14:4), while 'hiding the face' signifies the withdrawal of divine favor (Isa 54:8). Psalm 27 plays on this tension. The psalmist is commanded to 'Seek My face' (intimacy/favor), yet immediately pleads, 'Do not hide Your face' (rejection/abandonment). This vehicle transforms the abstract concept of 'favor' into an intense interpersonal encounter. The danger is not just the external 'army' (v3) but the potential loss of the Face. The 'gaze' (v4) upon beauty is the antidote to the 'hiding' of the face.

---

#### 5. Divine Adoption: Gathering the Abandoned
*Addresses verses*: 27:10  

In verse 10, the psalmist employs the vehicle of 'gathering' (*asaf*) in the context of parental abandonment. The concordance shows *asaf* is frequently used for death ('gathered to his people') or harvest. However, Zech 10:8 links it to a shepherd whistling for his flock. By juxtaposing the failure of the most primal human bonds ('father and mother forsake') with YHWH's 'gathering,' the poet redefines the term. It is neither death nor harvest, but adoption. YHWH acts as the ultimate kin-keeper, taking in the one whom society and family have cast off. This creates a powerful inclusion motif that answers the exclusion of being 'devoured' by enemies.

#### Vehicle: LIGHT (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:1** (metaphor)

*Hebrew phrase*: יְהֹוָ֤ה ׀ אוֹרִ֣י  
*Full verse*: לְדָוִ֨ד ׀ יְהֹוָ֤ה ׀ אוֹרִ֣י וְ֭יִשְׁעִי מִמִּ֣י אִירָ֑א יְהֹוָ֥ה מָעוֹז־חַ֝יַּ֗י מִמִּ֥י אֶפְחָֽד׃

**2. Job 38:2** (metaphor)  
*English*: darkens counsel  
*Hebrew phrase*: מַחְשִׁ֖יךְ עֵצָ֥ה

**4. 2 Kings 8:19** (metaphor)

*Hebrew phrase*: לָתֵ֨ת ל֥וֹ נִ֛יר לְבָנָ֖יו

**5. Psalms 139:12** (simile)

*Why selected*: Shows 'house' as a metaphor for a structured, secure way of life (Wisdom), paralleling the safety of YHWH's house.  

**4. Daniel 3:29** (metaphor)  
*English*: and their house confiscated (lit. made a dunghill)  
*Hebrew phrase*: וּבַיְתֵ֖הּ נְוָלִ֣י יִשְׁתַּוֵּ֑ה  
*Why selected*: Illustrates the total ruin of a house, highlighting the stakes of the psalmist's request for eternal dwelling.  

**5. Zechariah 3:7** (other)  
*English*: rule My House  
*Hebrew phrase*: תָּדִ֣ין אֶת־בֵּיתִ֔י  
*Full verse*: וְגַם־אַתָּה֙ תָּדִ֣ין אֶת־בֵּיתִ֔י וְגַ֖ם תִּשְׁמֹ֣ר אֶת־חֲצֵרָ֑י  
*Why selected*: Uses 'house' metonymically for the administration of worship, relevant to the psalmist's desire to 'inquire' in the temple.  

---

#### Vehicle: GAZE (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:4** (metaphor)  
*English*: to gaze upon the beauty  
*Hebrew phrase*: לַחֲז֥וֹת בְּנֹעַם־יְ֝הֹוָ֗ה  
*Full verse*: לַחֲז֥וֹת בְּנֹעַם־יְ֝הֹוָ֗ה וּלְבַקֵּ֥ר בְּהֵֽיכָלֽוֹ׃  
*Why selected*: The target text; implies a beatific vision or deep contemplation of divine favor.  

**2. Proverbs 22:12** (metaphor)  
*English*: The eyes of the LORD watch knowledge  
*Hebrew phrase*: עֵינֵי יְהוָה נָצְרוּ דָעַת  
*Full verse*: עֵינֵ֣י יְ֭הֹוָה נָ֣צְרוּ דָ֑עַת  
*Why selected*: Shows the reciprocal nature of gazing: while the psalmist gazes at God, God's eyes watch over truth.  

**3. Isaiah 17:8** (metaphor)  
*English*: look to the sacred posts  
*Hebrew phrase*: לֹא יִרְאֶה  
*Full verse*: וְלֹ֣א יִשְׁעֶ֔ה אֶל־הַֽמִּזְבְּח֖וֹת ... לֹ֣א יִרְאֶ֔ה  
*Why selected*: Contrasts the righteous gaze (at YHWH's beauty) with the idolatrous gaze (at man-made objects).  

**4. Proverbs 17:24** (metaphor)  
*English*: The eyes of the dullard range to the ends of the earth  
*Hebrew phrase*: וְעֵינֵי כְסִיל בִּקְצֵה־אָרֶץ  
*Full verse*: וְעֵינֵ֥י כְ֝סִ֗יל בִּקְצֵה־אָֽרֶץ׃  
*Why selected*: Contrasts the focused gaze of the psalmist ('One thing I ask') with the scattered gaze of the fool.  

**5. 2 Chronicles 20:12** (metaphor)  
*English*: but our eyes are on You  
*Hebrew phrase*: כִּ֥י עָלֶ֖יךָ עֵינֵֽינוּ  
*Full verse*: כִּ֥י עָלֶ֖יךָ עֵינֵֽינוּ׃  
*Why selected*: A perfect parallel to Ps 27, using the gaze as a metaphor for total dependence and expectation.  

---

#### Vehicle: SHELTER (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:5** (metaphor)  
*English*: He will shelter me in His pavilion  
*Hebrew phrase*: יִצְפְּנֵנִי בְּסֻכֹּה  
*Full verse*: כִּ֤י יִצְפְּנֵ֨נִי ׀ בְּסֻכֹּה֮ בְּי֢וֹם רָ֫עָ֥ה יַ֭סְתִּרֵנִי בְּסֵ֥תֶר אׇהֳל֑וֹ  
*Why selected*: The target text; uses 'sukkah' (booth), a fragile structure, as a paradoxically impregnable hiding place.  

**2. Psalms 91:1** (metaphor)  
*English*: in the shelter of the Most High  
*Hebrew phrase*: בְּסֵ֣תֶר עֶלְי֑וֹן  
*Full verse*: יֹ֭שֵׁב בְּסֵ֣תֶר עֶלְי֑וֹן בְּצֵ֥ל שַׁ֝דַּ֗י יִתְלוֹנָֽן׃  
*Why selected*: Reinforces the 'hiding' aspect of shelter (seter) found in Ps 27:5 ('yastireni b'seter').  

**3. Ezekiel 17:23** (metaphor)  
*English*: take shelter in the shade of its boughs  
*Hebrew phrase*: בְּצֵל דָּלִיּוֹתָיו תִּשְׁכֹּנָּה  
*Full verse*: וְשָׁכְנ֣וּ תַחְתָּ֗יו כֹּ֚ל צִפּ֣וֹר כׇּל־כָּנָ֔ף בְּצֵ֥ל דָּלִיּוֹתָ֖יו תִּשְׁכֹּֽנָּה׃  
*Why selected*: Connects shelter with 'shade', emphasizing relief from exposure.  

**4. Psalms 31:20** (metaphor)  
*English*: for those who take refuge in You  
*Hebrew phrase*: לַחֹסִ֣ים בָּ֑ךְ  
*Full verse*: פָּ֭עַלְתָּ לַחֹסִ֣ים בָּ֑ךְ נֶ֝֗גֶד בְּנֵ֣י אָדָֽם׃  
*Why selected*: Links the physical concept of shelter to the relational act of trust (chasah).  

**5. Isaiah 32:2** (simile)  
*English*: like the shade of a massive rock  
*Hebrew phrase*: כְּצֵל סֶלַע־כָּבֵד  
*Full verse*: כְּצֵ֥ל סֶלַע־כָּבֵ֖ד בְּאֶ֥רֶץ עֲיֵפָֽה׃  
*Why selected*: Bridges the imagery of 'shelter' and 'rock' (both in Ps 27:5), showing how a rock provides shelter.  

---

#### Vehicle: ROCK (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:5** (metaphor)  
*English*: set me high upon a rock  
*Hebrew phrase*: בְּ֝צ֗וּר יְרוֹמְמֵֽנִי  
*Full verse*: יַ֭סְתִּרֵנִי בְּסֵ֥תֶר אׇהֳל֑וֹ בְּ֝צ֗וּר יְרוֹמְמֵֽנִי׃  
*Why selected*: The target text; emphasizes vertical elevation and inaccessibility to enemies.  

**2. Deuteronomy 32:31** (simile)  
*English*: For their rock is not like our Rock  
*Hebrew phrase*: כִּ֛י לֹ֥א כְצוּרֵ֖נוּ צוּרָ֑ם  
*Full verse*: כִּ֛י לֹ֥א כְצוּרֵ֖נוּ צוּרָ֑ם וְאֹיְבֵ֖ינוּ פְּלִילִֽים׃  
*Why selected*: Contrasts the true source of stability (YHWH) with false securities, relevant to the 'false witnesses' in Ps 27.  

**3. Psalms 62:3** (metaphor)  
*English*: my rock  
*Hebrew phrase*: צ֭וּרִי  
*Full verse*: אַךְ־ה֣וּא צ֭וּרִי וִישׁוּעָתִ֑י מִ֝שְׂגַּבִּ֗י לֹא־אֶמּ֥וֹט רַבָּֽה׃  
*Why selected*: Pairs 'rock' with 'salvation' (yeshuah), mirroring Ps 27:1 ('light and salvation').  

**4. Jeremiah 5:3** (metaphor)  
*English*: They made their faces harder than rock  
*Hebrew phrase*: חִזְּקוּ פְנֵיהֶם מִסֶּלַע  
*Full verse*: חִזְּק֤וּ פְנֵיהֶם֙ מִסֶּ֔לַע מֵאֲנ֖וּ לָשֽׁוּב׃  
*Why selected*: Uses rock imagery for stubbornness, contrasting with the protective stability of the rock in Ps 27.  

**5. Job 29:6** (metaphor)  
*English*: rocks poured out streams of oil  
*Hebrew phrase*: וְצ֥וּר יָצ֥וּק ... פַּלְגֵי־שָֽׁמֶן  
*Full verse*: וְצ֥וּר יָצ֥וּק עִ֝מָּדִ֗י פַּלְגֵי־שָֽׁמֶן׃  
*Why selected*: Shows the rock as a source of supernatural provision, not just defense.  

---

#### Vehicle: HEAD (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:6** (metaphor)  
*English*: Now is my head high  
*Hebrew phrase*: וְעַתָּ֨ה יָר֪וּם רֹאשִׁ֡י  
*Full verse*: וְעַתָּ֨ה יָר֪וּם רֹאשִׁ֡י עַל אֹיְבַ֬י סְֽבִיבוֹתַ֗י  
*Why selected*: The target text; signifies victory, confidence, and restoration of honor over enemies.  

**2. Job 10:15** (metaphor)  
*English*: I cannot lift my head  
*Hebrew phrase*: לֹא־אֶשָּׂ֣א רֹאשִׁ֑י  
*Full verse*: וְ֭צָדַקְתִּי לֹא־אֶשָּׂ֣א רֹאשִׁ֑י שְׂבַ֥ע קָ֝ל֗וֹן וּרְאֵ֥ה עׇנְיִֽי׃  
*Why selected*: The inverse of Ps 27:6; shows that a lowered head signifies shame and guilt.  

**3. Jeremiah 23:19** (metaphor)  
*English*: whirl down upon the heads of the wicked  
*Hebrew phrase*: עַ֛ל רֹ֥אשׁ רְשָׁעִ֖ים יָחֽוּל  
*Full verse*: וְסַ֖עַר מִתְחוֹלֵ֑ל עַ֛ל רֹ֥אשׁ רְשָׁעִ֖ים יָחֽוּל׃  
*Why selected*: Shows the head as the locus of judgment for the wicked, while the righteous head is lifted (Ps 27).  

**4. Lamentations 3:54** (metaphor)  
*English*: Waters flowed over my head  
*Hebrew phrase*: צָֽפוּ־מַ֥יִם עַל־רֹאשִׁ֖י  
*Full verse*: צָֽפוּ־מַ֥יִם עַל־רֹאשִׁ֖י אָמַ֥רְתִּי נִגְזָֽרְתִּי׃  
*Why selected*: Illustrates the threat of being overwhelmed, which the 'lifted head' of Ps 27 overcomes.  

**5. Numbers 26:2** (idiom)  
*English*: Take a census (lit. lift the head)  
*Hebrew phrase*: שְׂא֞וּ אֶת־רֹ֣אשׁ  
*Full verse*: שְׂא֞וּ אֶת־רֹ֣אשׁ ׀ כׇּל־עֲדַ֣ת בְּנֵי־יִשְׂרָאֵ֗ל  
*Why selected*: Shows the idiomatic range of 'lifting the head' as counting/accounting, implying the psalmist is 'counted' among the living.  

---

#### Vehicle: FACE (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:8** (idiom)  
*English*: Seek My face!  
*Hebrew phrase*: בַּקְּשׁ֣וּ פָנָ֑י  
*Full verse*: לְךָ֤ ׀ אָמַ֣ר לִ֭בִּי בַּקְּשׁ֣וּ פָנָ֑י אֶת־פָּנֶ֖יךָ יְהֹוָ֣ה אֲבַקֵּֽשׁ׃  
*Why selected*: The target text; 'face' represents personal presence, favor, and direct relationship.  

**2. Isaiah 54:8** (metaphor)  
*English*: I hid My face from you  
*Hebrew phrase*: הִסְתַּ֨רְתִּי פָנַ֥י ... מִמֵּ֔ךְ  
*Full verse*: בְּשֶׁ֣צֶף קֶ֗צֶף הִסְתַּ֨רְתִּי פָנַ֥י רֶ֙גַע֙ מִמֵּ֔ךְ  
*Why selected*: Explains the fear in Ps 27:9 ('Do not hide Your face'); hiding the face is an idiom for withdrawing favor and protection.  

**3. Ezekiel 14:4** (metaphor)  
*English*: sets his mind (lit. face) upon the sin  
*Hebrew phrase*: יָשִׂים֙ נֹ֣כַח פָּנָ֔יו  
*Full verse*: וּמִכְשׁ֤וֹל עֲוֺנוֹ֙ יָשִׂים֙ נֹ֣כַח פָּנָ֔יו  
*Why selected*: Shows the negative use of 'face' (orientation toward sin), contrasting with the psalmist's orientation toward YHWH.  

**4. Job 42:8** (idiom)  
*English*: I will show favor (lit. lift his face)  
*Hebrew phrase*: כִּ֧י אִם־פָּנָ֣יו אֶשָּׂ֗א  
*Full verse*: כִּ֧י אִם־פָּנָ֣יו אֶשָּׂ֗א לְבִלְתִּ֞י עֲשׂ֤וֹת עִמָּכֶם֙ נְבָלָ֔ה  
*Why selected*: Demonstrates that 'lifting the face' means acceptance, illuminating the goal of seeking God's face in Ps 27.  

**5. Jeremiah 42:15** (metaphor)  
*English*: set your faces to go  
*Hebrew phrase*: שׂוֹם תְּשִׂימוּן פְּנֵיכֶם  
*Full verse*: אִם־אַ֠תֶּ֠ם שׂ֣וֹם תְּשִׂמ֤וּן פְּנֵיכֶם֙ לָבֹ֣א מִצְרַ֔יִם  
*Why selected*: Illustrates 'face' as the locus of will and determination.  

---

#### Vehicle: WAY (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:11** (metaphor)  
*English*: Show me Your way  
*Hebrew phrase*: ה֤וֹרֵ֥נִי יְהֹוָ֗ה דַּ֫רְכֶּ֥ךָ  
*Full verse*: ה֤וֹרֵ֥נִי יְהֹוָ֗ה דַּ֫רְכֶּ֥ךָ וּ֭נְחֵנִי בְּאֹ֣רַח מִישׁ֑וֹר  
*Why selected*: The target text; equates moral/spiritual instruction with a physical path.  

**2. Isaiah 55:9** (simile)  
*English*: My ways high above your ways  
*Hebrew phrase*: גָּבְה֤וּ דְרָכַי֙ מִדַּרְכֵיכֶ֔ם  
*Full verse*: כֵּ֣ן גָּבְה֤וּ דְרָכַי֙ מִדַּרְכֵיכֶ֔ם וּמַחְשְׁבֹתַ֖י מִמַּחְשְׁבֹֽתֵיכֶֽם׃  
*Why selected*: Highlights the transcendence of God's 'way', explaining why the psalmist needs to be 'taught' it.  

**3. Proverbs 2:8** (metaphor)  
*English*: protecting the way of His loyal ones  
*Hebrew phrase*: וְדֶ֖רֶךְ חֲסִידָ֣ו יִשְׁמֹֽר  
*Full verse*: לִ֭נְצֹר אׇרְח֣וֹת מִשְׁפָּ֑ט וְדֶ֖רֶךְ חֲסִידָ֣ו יִשְׁמֹֽר׃  
*Why selected*: Connects the 'way' to divine protection, linking the petition in v11 back to the confidence in v1.  

**4. Jeremiah 7:5** (metaphor)  
*English*: mend your ways  
*Hebrew phrase*: תֵּיטִ֔יבוּ אֶת־דַּרְכֵיכֶ֖ם  
*Full verse*: כִּ֤י אִם־הֵיטֵיב֙ תֵּיטִ֔יבוּ אֶת־דַּרְכֵיכֶ֖ם וְאֶת־מַעַלְלֵיכֶ֑ם  
*Why selected*: Shows 'way' as habitual moral conduct that can be corrected.  

**5. Proverbs 28:18** (metaphor)  
*English*: whose ways are crooked  
*Hebrew phrase*: וְנֶעְקַ֥שׁ דְּ֝רָכַ֗יִם  
*Full verse*: וְנֶעְקַ֥שׁ דְּ֝רָכַ֗יִם יִפּ֥וֹל בְּאֶחָֽת׃  
*Why selected*: Contrasts with the 'level path' (orach mishor) requested in Ps 27:11.  

---

#### Vehicle: SHIELD (5 curated examples)

**1. Genesis 15:1** (metaphor)  
*English*: I am a shield to you  
*Hebrew phrase*: אָנֹכִי֙ מָגֵ֣ן לָ֔ךְ  
*Full verse*: אַל־תִּירָ֣א אַבְרָ֗ם אָנֹכִי֙ מָגֵ֣ן לָ֔ךְ שְׂכָרְךָ֖ הַרְבֵּ֥ה מְאֹֽד׃  
*Why selected*: A foundational metaphor for God as personal protection, thematically linked to Ps 27's 'stronghold'.  

**2. Psalms 3:4** (metaphor)  
*English*: a shield about me  
*Hebrew phrase*: מָגֵ֣ן בַּעֲדִ֑י  
*Full verse*: וְאַתָּ֣ה יְ֭הֹוָה מָגֵ֣ן בַּעֲדִ֑י כְּ֝בוֹדִ֗י וּמֵרִ֥ים רֹאשִֽׁי׃  
*Why selected*: Links 'shield' with 'lifting the head', a combination found in Ps 27 (Stronghold + Head lifted).  

**3. Psalms 5:13** (simile)  
*English*: encompassing him with favor like a shield  
*Hebrew phrase*: כַּ֝צִּנָּ֗ה רָצ֥וֹן תַּעְטְרֶֽנּוּ  
*Full verse*: כִּֽי־אַתָּה֮ תְּבָרֵ֢ךְ צַ֫דִּ֥יק יְהֹוָ֑ה כַּ֝צִּנָּ֗ה רָצ֥וֹן תַּעְטְרֶֽנּוּ׃  
*Why selected*: Compares divine favor (ratzon) to a large shield (tzinnah), illuminating the 'shelter' imagery.  

**4. Psalms 84:10** (metaphor)  
*English*: our shield  
*Hebrew phrase*: מָ֭גִנֵּנוּ  
*Full verse*: מָ֭גִנֵּנוּ רְאֵ֣ה אֱלֹהִ֑ים וְ֝הַבֵּ֗ט פְּנֵ֣י מְשִׁיחֶֽךָ׃  
*Why selected*: Identifies the king/anointed as a shield, relevant to the Davidic context of Ps 27.  

**5. Psalms 91:4** (metaphor)  
*English*: encircling shield  
*Hebrew phrase*: צִנָּ֖ה וְסֹחֵרָ֣ה  
*Full verse*: צִנָּ֖ה וְסֹחֵרָ֣ה אֲמִתּֽוֹ׃  
*Why selected*: Pairs shield with truth/faithfulness, similar to the moral dimensions of protection in Ps 27.  

---

#### Vehicle: WING (5 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 63:8** (metaphor)  
*English*: in the shadow of Your wings  
*Hebrew phrase*: וּבְצֵ֖ל כְּנָפֶ֣יךָ  
*Full verse*: כִּֽי־הָיִ֣יתָ עֶזְרָ֣תָה לִּ֑י וּבְצֵ֖ל כְּנָפֶ֣יךָ אֲרַנֵּֽן׃  
*Why selected*: A parallel metaphor for protection ('shadow') found in Ps 27:5 ('shelter/tent'), though Ps 27 uses architectural rather than avian imagery.  

**2. Psalms 91:4** (metaphor)  
*English*: Your protecting wings  
*Hebrew phrase*: כְּנָפֶ֣יךָ  
*Full verse*: בְּאֶבְרָת֨וֹ ׀ יָ֣סֶךְ לָ֭ךְ וְתַחַת־כְּנָפָ֣יו תֶּחְסֶ֑ה  
*Why selected*: Explicitly links wings to 'taking refuge' (chasah), a concept implied in Ps 27's 'shelter'.  

**3. Malachi 3:20** (metaphor)  
*English*: healing in its wings  
*Hebrew phrase*: וּמַרְפֵּ֖א בִּכְנָפֶ֑יהָ  
*Full verse*: וְזָרְחָ֨ה לָכֶ֜ם ... שֶׁ֣מֶשׁ צְדָקָ֔ה וּמַרְפֵּ֖א בִּכְנָפֶ֑יהָ  
*Why selected*: Combines 'sun' (light) and 'wings', linking the 'Light' of Ps 27:1 with protective imagery.  

**4. Jeremiah 48:40** (simile)  
*English*: spreads out his wings against Moab  
*Hebrew phrase*: וּפָרַ֥שׂ כְּנָפָ֖יו אֶל־מוֹאָֽב  
*Full verse*: הִנֵּ֥ה כַנֶּ֖שֶׁר יִדְאֶ֑ה וּפָרַ֥שׂ כְּנָפָ֖יו אֶל־מוֹאָֽב׃  
*Why selected*: Shows wings as a threat (predatory eagle), contrasting with the protective wings of God.  

**5. Psalms 139:9** (metaphor)  
*English*: wings of the dawn  
*Hebrew phrase*: כַנְפֵי־שָׁ֑חַר  
*Full verse*: אֶשָּׂ֥א כַנְפֵי־שָׁ֑חַר אֶ֝שְׁכְּנָ֗ה בְּאַחֲרִ֥ית יָֽם׃  
*Why selected*: Personifies light (dawn) with wings, bridging the 'Light' and 'Wing' motifs.  

---

#### Vehicle: LION (5 curated examples)

**1. Jeremiah 12:8** (simile)  
*English*: like a lion in the forest  
*Hebrew phrase*: כְּאַרְיֵ֣ה בַיָּ֑עַר  
*Full verse*: הָיְתָה־לִּ֥י נַחֲלָתִ֖י כְּאַרְיֵ֣ה בַיָּ֑עַר נָתְנָ֥ה עָלַ֛י בְּקוֹלָ֖הּ  
*Why selected*: Depicts the enemy as a roaring lion, illuminating the 'devouring' enemies of Ps 27:2.  

**2. Psalms 34:11** (metaphor)  
*English*: Lions have been reduced to starvation  
*Hebrew phrase*: כְּ֭פִירִים רָשׁ֣וּ וְרָעֵ֑בוּ  
*Full verse*: כְּ֭פִירִים רָשׁ֣וּ וְרָעֵ֑בוּ וְדֹרְשֵׁ֥י יְ֝הֹוָ֗ה לֹא־יַחְסְר֥וּ כׇל־טֽוֹב׃  
*Why selected*: Contrasts the self-reliant 'lions' (wicked) with those who seek YHWH (like the psalmist in Ps 27).  

**3. Isaiah 38:13** (simile)  
*English*: Like a lion, so he breaks all my bones  
*Hebrew phrase*: כָּאֲרִ֔י כֵּ֥ן יְשַׁבֵּ֖ר כׇּל־עַצְמוֹתָ֑י  
*Full verse*: שִׁוִּ֤יתִי עַד־בֹּ֙קֶר֙ כָּאֲרִ֔י כֵּ֥ן יְשַׁבֵּ֖ר כׇּל־עַצְמוֹתָ֑י  
*Why selected*: Provides vivid imagery for the 'devouring of flesh' mentioned in Ps 27:2.  

**4. Proverbs 22:13** (hyperbole)  
*English*: There’s a lion in the street  
*Hebrew phrase*: אֲרִ֣י בַח֑וּץ  
*Full verse*: אָמַ֣ר עָ֭צֵל אֲרִ֣י בַח֑וּץ בְּת֥וֹךְ רְ֝חֹב֗וֹת אֵרָצֵֽחַ׃  
*Why selected*: Illustrates the paralyzing fear of enemies, which Ps 27:1 ('Whom shall I fear?') overcomes.  

**5. Nahum 2:13** (metaphor)  
*English*: lion that tore victims  
*Hebrew phrase*: אַרְיֵ֤ה טֹרֵף֙  
*Full verse*: אַרְיֵ֤ה טֹרֵף֙ בְּדֵ֣י גֹרוֹתָ֔יו ... וַיְמַלֵּא־טֶ֣רֶף חֹרָ֔יו  
*Why selected*: Uses 'tearing' (toref) imagery, cognate to the 'devouring' (eating) of flesh in Ps 27.  

---

#### Vehicle: GATHER (3 curated examples)

**1. Psalms 27:10** (metaphor)  
*English*: the LORD will take me in (gather me)  
*Hebrew phrase*: וַֽיהֹוָ֣ה יַאַסְפֵֽנִי  
*Full verse*: כִּֽי־אָבִ֣י וְאִמִּ֣י עֲזָב֑וּנִי וַֽיהֹוָ֣ה יַאַסְפֵֽנִי׃  
*Why selected*: The target text; redefines 'gathering' from a harvest/death term to one of adoption and care.  

**2. Zechariah 10:8** (metaphor)  
*English*: I will whistle to them and gather them  
*Hebrew phrase*: אֶשְׁרְקָ֥ה לָהֶ֛ם וַאֲקַבְּצֵ֖ם  
*Full verse*: אֶשְׁרְקָ֥ה לָהֶ֛ם וַאֲקַבְּצֵ֖ם כִּ֣י פְדִיתִ֑ים  
*Why selected*: Depicts gathering as a shepherd's act, suggesting YHWH acts as the parent/shepherd when human parents fail.  

**3. Job 30:23** (metaphor)  
*English*: The house assigned (of gathering) for all the living  
*Hebrew phrase*: וּבֵ֖ית מוֹעֵ֣ד לְכׇל־חָֽי  
*Full verse*: כִּֽי־יָ֭דַעְתִּי מָ֣וֶת תְּשִׁיבֵ֑נִי וּבֵ֖ית מוֹעֵ֣ד לְכׇל־חָֽי׃  
*Why selected*: Contrasts the 'gathering' of death with the 'gathering' of YHWH in Ps 27:10 (life/adoption).

## Scholarly Context (RAG Documents)

### Psalm 27 Function & Genre
**Genre**: Wisdom Psalm  

**Structure**:  
- v. 1-3: Confession of trust in the Lord amid fear  
- v. 4-6: Desire to dwell in the house of the Lord  
- v. 7-10: Prayers for mercy and affirmation of God’s protection  
- v. 11-14: Final exhortation to trust in the Lord and be of good courage  

**Keywords**: trust, courage, worship, salvation, protection
